HUNT
INFO
Dear hunters,
Best of luck to you as you embark on your 2026 big game hunting season. We appreciate you taking the time to
understand the rules and regulations for this year’s hunt. These regulations are the result of careful planning and
research from our personnel, as well as community feedback through a rigorous commenting and public meeting
process.
Hunting in Wyoming is a collaborative and ongoing effort, with each member playing an essential role.
Before our annual seasons are even established, we rely on the public comment and feedback to develop
meaningful and effective regulations each year. Hunters who respect the lands they use, the wildlife they hunt,
and the regulations that our state provides ensure that access and hunting can continue.
Agency initiatives such as the Mule Deer Conservation Strategy or the Statewide Habitat Plan provide cohesive
paths forward in wildlife management and conservation. We are fortunate to have a state where residents
understand the importance of the work we do, and play an active role in our conservation efforts, and landowners
who work tirelessly to provide good habitat and hunting access help ensure management success and resources .
Whether through providing public comment, fundraising, volunteering, or purchasing licenses, conservation
stamps, and other products that support our efforts, the hunting community is home to our strongest conservation
leaders, and the leaders of tomorrow.
Thank you for continuing to support the outdoor spirit of Wyoming and the traditions that come with it. We
appreciate your adherence to our regulations, and I wish you and your families the best of luck for your 2026 big
game hunting season.
Sincerely,
Angi Bruce
Director, Wyoming Game and Fish Department
DISCLAIMER
The following sections are a condensed summary of applicable wildlife laws and other important information. This summary is in no way intended to
alter the content or statutory intent of those laws. If you have any questions regarding the precise language, you can view a copy of the State Stat-
utes (Title 23) and Wyoming Game and Fish Commission (Commission) Regulations, available at the Cheyenne Headquarters, Department Regional
Offices, the Department website or consult the following websites: https://rules.wyo.gov or https://wyoleg.gov.
CARD FEE
The Department shall charge a two point five percent (2.5%) processing fee of the cost of any license, permit, stamp, tag, preference point or com-
petitive raffle chance applied for or purchased by the use of a debit or credit card.

CONSERVATION STAMP
Don’t Forget your Conservation Stamp!
Hunters and anglers must purchase a conservation stamp
prior to hunting and fishing in Wyoming.
Conservation stamps are only available electronically through the Department’s Electronic Licensing
System (ELS), and may be purchased from the Cheyenne Headquarters, Department Regional
Offices, license selling agents throughout the state and the Department website.
EXEMPTIONS
Persons holding the following licenses are exempt from the requirement to pur-
• Resident United States Military Purple Heart Medal recipients.
chase a conservation stamp while exercising hunting or fishing privileges under
ANY Wyoming license. The person shall, at all times, be in possession of the license Persons holding the following licenses are exempt from the requirement to pur-
allowing the conservation stamp exemption while in the field: chase a conservation stamp while exercising privileges under THESE licenses:
• Daily hunting or fishing license;
• Any Wyoming pioneer hunting or fishing license; • Nonresident five (5) consecutive day fishing license;
• Honorably discharged Wyoming resident veteran who is one hundred percent • Special limited fishing permit holders;
(100%) disabled game bird, small game and fishing license; • Wyoming fifty (50%) percent disabled veteran fishing license; or,
• Military combat general elk or general deer license; • Resident permanently and totally disabled lifetime fishing license.
• Military combat game bird or small game license;
• Licenses reissued to a veteran with disabilities or a person with a permanent A lifetime conservation stamp may be purchased by applying to the Cheyenne Head-
disability who uses a wheelchair; or, quarters or through any Department Regional Office.
Every hunting license bought, every fishing license sold, and every
acre of private land managed for wildlife is a direct investment in
our state’s most valuable asset: our habitat.
Invest in the future
of our terrestrial and
aquatic habitats.

er state for disposal in an approved landfill or incinerator.
Transportation and disposal shall be in accordance with
that state’s laws, rules and regulations.
d. Nothing in this section shall apply to the transportation or disposal
of deer, elk and moose taken by any governmental agency or edu-
cational institution.

loins and tenderloins, excluding meat on the ribs and neck.

A shared heritage — Great habitat doesn’t
happen by accident; it happens by partnership.
From the highest timbered ridges to the lowest river valleys, Wyoming’s wildlife
thrives because of a shared commitment by hunters, anglers and landowners to
steward the critical winter ranges and riparian corridors.
Learn more about habitat conservation at wgfd.info/habitat

Get to know bears in Wyoming
Wyoming is home to black bears and grizzly bears. It’s dangerous and being able to identify the species can help
important to know the difference between the two whether you stay safe in bear country. When identifying a bear, make
you are a hunter or an occasional hiker. Although grizzly sure to use all four reliable characteristics to determine the
bears are often more aggressive, black bears can still be species. Never use color or size to identify a bear.

Dating and Display
When any big game animal, trophy
game animal or wild turkey is killed un-
der a license, or sandhill crane is killed
under a limited quota sandhill crane
permit, the licensee shall detach,
sign and date the proper carcass
coupon and attach the coupon to the
carcass in a readily visible manner
before leaving the site of the kill.
CUT OUT the entire DAY of the kill.
When dating a carcass coupon, the
entire day and month of the kill shall
be completely cut out and removed.
The carcass coupon shall remain on
the game animal, wild turkey or sandhill
crane carcass at all times until the meat
undergoes processing, or on the trophy
game animal hide until it reaches the
hunter’s home or a taxidermist, except
that during transportation of the car-
cass or hide, the validated carcass
coupon may be removed to prevent
its loss. If the carcass coupon is re-
moved for transportation of the carcass
or hide, it must be completely filled out SIGN the carcass coupon.
and in the possession of the person
accompanying the carcass or hide at
all times. While quarters or pieces of an
animal are being packed from the field,
the carcass coupon shall remain with
the person transporting the animal.

114 1 Aug. 15 Sep. 30 Oct. 1 Oct. 31 50 Any antelope which usually lack visible horns, the black cheek patches distinguish
males from females. Females lack the black cheek patch.

Section 3. Special Archery Seasons
a. There shall be special archery antelope hunting seasons on the dates specified in Section 2.
Special archery hunting seasons are specific to individual hunt areas by license type. During
the special archery hunting seasons, archers are restricted to the limitations associated with
the special archery season dates for a hunt area and license type.
b. Archers shall possess a limited quota antelope license and an archery license in order to hunt
1
antelope with archery equipment during any special archery season.
2
i. Type 9 limited quota antelope licenses are valid for “archery only” and do not require a 2
separate archery license.
ii. Type 0 limited quota antelope licenses are valid for specialized hunting equipment, such
as muzzle-loading firearms or handguns, and are not valid for use with archery equipment
during any special archery antelope seasons.
Section 4. Hunting Season Extension Permits
a. Any person qualified for and in possession of a Hunting Season Extension Permit issued by
the Department in accordance with Commission regulations may hunt antelope starting five
Yearling buck
(5) days prior to the earliest opening regular season date in the hunt area(s) for which their
license and type is valid, and is restricted to the limitations of their license type for the earliest 1
opening regular season date as specified in Section 2 of this Chapter.
b. Hunters participating in a hunting season extension shall be in possession of a Hunting Sea-
son Extension Permit which shall be immediately produced for inspection upon request of an 2
officer authorized to enforce this regulation.

GENERAL HUNTING INFORMATION
3
WGFD.WYO.GOV
HUNT
INFO
Dear hunters,
Best of luck to you as you embark on your 2026 big game hunting season. We appreciate you taking the time to
understand the rules and regulations for this year’s hunt. These regulations are the result of careful planning and
research from our personnel, as well as community feedback through a rigorous commenting and public meeting
process.
Hunting in Wyoming is a collaborative and ongoing effort, with each member playing an essential role.
Before our annual seasons are even established, we rely on the public comment and feedback to develop
meaningful and effective regulations each year. Hunters who respect the lands they use, the wildlife they hunt,
and the regulations that our state provides ensure that access and hunting can continue.
Agency initiatives such as the Mule Deer Conservation Strategy or the Statewide Habitat Plan provide cohesive
paths forward in wildlife management and conservation. We are fortunate to have a state where residents
understand the importance of the work we do, and play an active role in our conservation efforts, and landowners
who work tirelessly to provide good habitat and hunting access help ensure management success and resources .
Whether through providing public comment, fundraising, volunteering, or purchasing licenses, conservation
stamps, and other products that support our efforts, the hunting community is home to our strongest conservation
leaders, and the leaders of tomorrow.
Thank you for continuing to support the outdoor spirit of Wyoming and the traditions that come with it. We
appreciate your adherence to our regulations, and I wish you and your families the best of luck for your 2026 big
game hunting season.
Sincerely,
Angi Bruce
Director, Wyoming Game and Fish Department
DISCLAIMER
The following sections are a condensed summary of applicable wildlife laws and other important information. This summary is in no way intended to
alter the content or statutory intent of those laws. If you have any questions regarding the precise language, you can view a copy of the State Stat-
utes (Title 23) and Wyoming Game and Fish Commission (Commission) Regulations, available at the Cheyenne Headquarters, Department Regional
Offices, the Department website or consult the following websites: https://rules.wyo.gov or https://wyoleg.gov.
CARD FEE
The Department shall charge a two point five percent (2.5%) processing fee of the cost of any license, permit, stamp, tag, preference point or com-
petitive raffle chance applied for or purchased by the use of a debit or credit card.

CONSERVATION STAMP
Don’t Forget your Conservation Stamp!
Hunters and anglers must purchase a conservation stamp
prior to hunting and fishing in Wyoming.
Conservation stamps are only available electronically through the Department’s Electronic Licensing
System (ELS), and may be purchased from the Cheyenne Headquarters, Department Regional
Offices, license selling agents throughout the state and the Department website.
EXEMPTIONS
Persons holding the following licenses are exempt from the requirement to pur-
• Resident United States Military Purple Heart Medal recipients.
chase a conservation stamp while exercising hunting or fishing privileges under
ANY Wyoming license. The person shall, at all times, be in possession of the license Persons holding the following licenses are exempt from the requirement to pur-
allowing the conservation stamp exemption while in the field: chase a conservation stamp while exercising privileges under THESE licenses:
• Daily hunting or fishing license;
• Any Wyoming pioneer hunting or fishing license; • Nonresident five (5) consecutive day fishing license;
• Honorably discharged Wyoming resident veteran who is one hundred percent • Special limited fishing permit holders;
(100%) disabled game bird, small game and fishing license; • Wyoming fifty (50%) percent disabled veteran fishing license; or,
• Military combat general elk or general deer license; • Resident permanently and totally disabled lifetime fishing license.
• Military combat game bird or small game license;
• Licenses reissued to a veteran with disabilities or a person with a permanent A lifetime conservation stamp may be purchased by applying to the Cheyenne Head-
disability who uses a wheelchair; or, quarters or through any Department Regional Office.
Every hunting license bought, every fishing license sold, and every
acre of private land managed for wildlife is a direct investment in
our state’s most valuable asset: our habitat.
Invest in the future
of our terrestrial and
aquatic habitats.

A shared heritage — Great habitat doesn’t
happen by accident; it happens by partnership.
From the highest timbered ridges to the lowest river valleys, Wyoming’s wildlife
thrives because of a shared commitment by hunters, anglers and landowners to
steward the critical winter ranges and riparian corridors.
Learn more about habitat conservation at wgfd.info/habitat

Get to know bears in Wyoming
Wyoming is home to black bears and grizzly bears. It’s dangerous and being able to identify the species can help
important to know the difference between the two whether you stay safe in bear country. When identifying a bear, make
you are a hunter or an occasional hiker. Although grizzly sure to use all four reliable characteristics to determine the
bears are often more aggressive, black bears can still be species. Never use color or size to identify a bear.

HARVESTING AND BEFORE
from the license.
LEAVING THE SITE OF THE
KILL COMPLETE ALL OF
THE FOLLOWING STEPS:
CUT OUT the entire MONTH of the kill.
Carcass Coupons;
Dating and Display
When any big game animal, trophy
game animal or wild turkey is killed un-
der a license, or sandhill crane is killed
under a limited quota sandhill crane
permit, the licensee shall detach,
sign and date the proper carcass
coupon and attach the coupon to the
carcass in a readily visible manner
before leaving the site of the kill.
CUT OUT the entire DAY of the kill.
When dating a carcass coupon, the
entire day and month of the kill shall
be completely cut out and removed.
The carcass coupon shall remain on
the game animal, wild turkey or sandhill
crane carcass at all times until the meat
undergoes processing, or on the trophy
game animal hide until it reaches the
hunter’s home or a taxidermist, except
that during transportation of the car-
cass or hide, the validated carcass
coupon may be removed to prevent
its loss. If the carcass coupon is re-
moved for transportation of the carcass
or hide, it must be completely filled out SIGN the carcass coupon.
and in the possession of the person
accompanying the carcass or hide at
all times. While quarters or pieces of an
animal are being packed from the field,
the carcass coupon shall remain with
the person transporting the animal.

OUR COMMITMENT TO
CONSERVING MULE DEER
We all see the declines in mule deer. Game and Fish is putting
your license dollars to work to benefit mule deer populations
and habitat so hunting can occur for generations to come.
Listening to the Field: Through recent public meetings and surveys, your
on-the-ground insights are shaping our new management strategies with
over 45 public comments for deer submitted for the 2026 hunting season.
Clearing the Path: We continue to work diligently to convert hundreds of
miles of outdated fence into wildlife-friendly fencing to drastically reduce
entanglement and exhaustion.
Tracking the Truth: Guesswork doesn’t save herds. We are deploying GPS
collars on over 1,185 deer across the state and conducting comprehensive
research to pinpoint exactly where deer are struggling and why.
Conserving mule deer takes all of us. Track our current projects, see
the data, and learn how to get involved at wgfd.info/muledeer

G
MULE DEER NEED US NOW
E
T
I
It is no secret that our mule deer are facing tough times. However, without the daily, on-the-
N
ground management by the Game and Fish Department and commitments from numerous
V
partners, the current decline would be more severe.
O
Now, to truly turn the tide, we need to go on the offensive. Our best chance at mule deer
L
conservation is through coordinated, strategic conservation work that moves us to shared
V
stewardship of mule deer.
E
We are teaming up with external partners to secure the vital policy changes and essential D
funding that effective conservation requires.
Take Action: Read the Mule Deer Conservation Strategy online today to
see what it would take to conserve mule deer in Wyoming. We invite you to
bring your voice, expertise, and ideas to the upcoming Conservation Forum
to help shape the future of our herds.

GENERAL HUNTING INFORMATION

WGFD.WYO.GOV
HUNT
INFO
Dear hunters,
Best of luck to you as you embark on your 2026 big game hunting season. We appreciate you taking the time to
understand the rules and regulations for this year’s hunt. These regulations are the result of careful planning and
research from our personnel, as well as community feedback through a rigorous commenting and public meeting
process.
Hunting in Wyoming is a collaborative and ongoing effort, with each member playing an essential role.
Before our annual seasons are even established, we rely on the public comment and feedback to develop
meaningful and effective regulations each year. Hunters who respect the lands they use, the wildlife they hunt,
and the regulations that our state provides ensure that access and hunting can continue.
Agency initiatives such as the Mule Deer Conservation Strategy or the Statewide Habitat Plan provide cohesive
paths forward in wildlife management and conservation. We are fortunate to have a state where residents
understand the importance of the work we do, and play an active role in our conservation efforts, and landowners
who work tirelessly to provide good habitat and hunting access help ensure management success and resources .
Whether through providing public comment, fundraising, volunteering, or purchasing licenses, conservation
stamps, and other products that support our efforts, the hunting community is home to our strongest conservation
leaders, and the leaders of tomorrow.
Thank you for continuing to support the outdoor spirit of Wyoming and the traditions that come with it. We
appreciate your adherence to our regulations, and I wish you and your families the best of luck for your 2026 big
game hunting season.
Sincerely,
Angi Bruce
Director, Wyoming Game and Fish Department
DISCLAIMER
The following sections are a condensed summary of applicable wildlife laws and other important information. This summary is in no way intended to
alter the content or statutory intent of those laws. If you have any questions regarding the precise language, you can view a copy of the State Stat-
utes (Title 23) and Wyoming Game and Fish Commission (Commission) Regulations, available at the Cheyenne Headquarters, Department Regional
Offices, the Department website or consult the following websites: https://rules.wyo.gov or https://wyoleg.gov.
CARD FEE
The Department shall charge a two point five percent (2.5%) processing fee of the cost of any license, permit, stamp, tag, preference point or com-
petitive raffle chance applied for or purchased by the use of a debit or credit card.

CONSERVATION STAMP
Don’t Forget your Conservation Stamp!
Hunters and anglers must purchase a conservation stamp
prior to hunting and fishing in Wyoming.
Conservation stamps are only available electronically through the Department’s Electronic Licensing
System (ELS), and may be purchased from the Cheyenne Headquarters, Department Regional
Offices, license selling agents throughout the state and the Department website.
EXEMPTIONS
Persons holding the following licenses are exempt from the requirement to pur-
• Resident United States Military Purple Heart Medal recipients.
chase a conservation stamp while exercising hunting or fishing privileges under
ANY Wyoming license. The person shall, at all times, be in possession of the license Persons holding the following licenses are exempt from the requirement to pur-
allowing the conservation stamp exemption while in the field: chase a conservation stamp while exercising privileges under THESE licenses:
• Daily hunting or fishing license;
• Any Wyoming pioneer hunting or fishing license; • Nonresident five (5) consecutive day fishing license;
• Honorably discharged Wyoming resident veteran who is one hundred percent • Special limited fishing permit holders;
(100%) disabled game bird, small game and fishing license; • Wyoming fifty (50%) percent disabled veteran fishing license; or,
• Military combat general elk or general deer license; • Resident permanently and totally disabled lifetime fishing license.
• Military combat game bird or small game license;
• Licenses reissued to a veteran with disabilities or a person with a permanent A lifetime conservation stamp may be purchased by applying to the Cheyenne Head-
disability who uses a wheelchair; or, quarters or through any Department Regional Office.
Every hunting license bought, every fishing license sold, and every
acre of private land managed for wildlife is a direct investment in
our state’s most valuable asset: our habitat.
Invest in the future
of our terrestrial and
aquatic habitats.

A shared heritage — Great habitat doesn’t
happen by accident; it happens by partnership.
From the highest timbered ridges to the lowest river valleys, Wyoming’s wildlife
thrives because of a shared commitment by hunters, anglers and landowners to
steward the critical winter ranges and riparian corridors.
Learn more about habitat conservation at wgfd.info/habitat

Get to know bears in Wyoming
Wyoming is home to black bears and grizzly bears. It’s dangerous and being able to identify the species can help
important to know the difference between the two whether you stay safe in bear country. When identifying a bear, make
you are a hunter or an occasional hiker. Although grizzly sure to use all four reliable characteristics to determine the
bears are often more aggressive, black bears can still be species. Never use color or size to identify a bear.

Precautions When Hunting In Areas Occupied

Dating and Display
When any big game animal, trophy
game animal or wild turkey is killed un-
der a license, or sandhill crane is killed
under a limited quota sandhill crane
permit, the licensee shall detach,
sign and date the proper carcass
coupon and attach the coupon to the
carcass in a readily visible manner
before leaving the site of the kill.
CUT OUT the entire DAY of the kill.
When dating a carcass coupon, the
entire day and month of the kill shall
be completely cut out and removed.
The carcass coupon shall remain on
the game animal, wild turkey or sandhill
crane carcass at all times until the meat
undergoes processing, or on the trophy
game animal hide until it reaches the
hunter’s home or a taxidermist, except
that during transportation of the car-
cass or hide, the validated carcass
coupon may be removed to prevent
its loss. If the carcass coupon is re-
moved for transportation of the carcass
or hide, it must be completely filled out SIGN the carcass coupon.
and in the possession of the person
accompanying the carcass or hide at
all times. While quarters or pieces of an
animal are being packed from the field,
the carcass coupon shall remain with
the person transporting the animal.
